Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Um, well, my boss kind of made me come. It's just... what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service. Or, like, doing things totally out of order? The other day I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. 

Which is annoying because I usually always close my checks right away. But that cash drawer key, it has this really tight coiled plastic bracelet on it, you know the kind? Like a telephone cord. It always pulls the little hairs on my wrist, so I refuse to wear it, I just hold it in my hand. My old manager, Dave, he used to make us wear them on our wrists no matter what, but he transferred to the Peoria location last year. I actually lived in Peoria for a bit when I was taking classes at the community college there. I was going to do early childhood education, but the drive was just too far in the winter. Do you get a lot of snow where you live? Because driving in it is just the worst, the salt ruins the undercarriage of your car. 

But, um, anyway, yeah, the restaurant said I had to come see someone because of the... the freezing up thing, before they put me back on the schedule.

Um, I'm not really sure. I don't know if I lost time or not. It's hard to tell because we aren't allowed to wear watches on the floor anyway. It's a health code thing, you know? They say bacteria gets trapped under the watch band. Which makes sense, I guess, especially if you get salad dressing on it. Ranch dressing is the absolute worst to wash out of things. We make ours from scratch in the back, in these giant plastic buckets, and the buttermilk smell just lingers on your apron forever. 

My mom used to use buttermilk to make biscuits every Sunday, but she used the canned ones. You know, the kind that pop when you peel the paper back? I always hated opening those, the popping sound always made me jump so bad. I'd always make my brother do it. 

But, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, being aware. Yeah, I don't know, I just remember standing there with the key. I don't really know what was happening around me.

Um, I don't really know if it's random or not. I haven't really noticed a pattern, I guess. I mean, it happened that one time by the soda station, but the soda station is always so chaotic anyway. People are always bumping into you trying to get ice. We have this one machine that dispenses crushed ice instead of cubed, and everyone fights over it because the customers always specifically ask for crushed ice in their iced teas. 

I actually prefer crushed ice myself, especially when I'm drinking lemonade. My grandma used to make the best homemade lemonade in the summer. She had this huge lemon tree in her backyard, and we'd spend hours picking them. She had this weird little metal contraption to squeeze them, and it always pinched my fingers if I wasn't careful. She kept it in this drawer with all her other random kitchen gadgets, like an apple corer and a melon baller. Do people even use melon ballers anymore? I feel like you only ever see those fancy fruit salads at weddings or maybe baby showers. 

My cousin just had a baby shower last month, and they had this huge fruit carving that looked like a bassinet. It was actually kind of creepy looking, to be honest. The baby's head was made out of a cantaloupe. 

But, um... yeah, I don't know about the freezing thing. I guess it just happens whenever. I haven't really paid attention to what's going on around me when it starts.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I don't really think it has anything to do with Dave, or the bracelet. I mean, the bracelet is just really annoying. It's one of those plastic coil ones, kind of like an old telephone cord? We had this bright yellow phone on the wall in our kitchen growing up, with a cord that stretched all the way into the living room so my mom could watch TV while she talked on it. She loved watching those daytime soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having secret evil twins. I always thought it was so funny how the actors would just stare off into the distance dramatically before the commercial breaks. 

Like, nobody actually does that in real life. Unless they're trying to read a menu that's really far away, I guess. We have this big chalkboard menu above the register at work, and the chalk markers they use are so faded you can barely read the daily soup specials. I keep telling Dave we need to buy new ones, but he's super cheap about office supplies. He won't even buy the good pens, just those cheap clear plastic ones where the ink gets all clumpy at the tip and smears all over your hands. If you get that black ink on your uniform shirt, it is never coming out, no matter how much hairspray you use on it. People always say hairspray gets out ink, but I think that's just an old wives' tale. My aunt used to swear by club soda for stains, but club soda just tastes like angry water to me. 

But, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing. Yeah, I haven't noticed it happening more around Dave or when I look at the bracelet or anything. It doesn't really seem connected to that.

He won't even buy the good pens, just those cheap clear plastic ones where the ink gets all clumpy at the tip and smears all over your hands. Um, well, it's mostly at work, I think. That's actually what scared everyone there, honestly. I would just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or I'd do things totally out of order. Like, I'd be standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. 

The cash drawer key is on this really long, annoying lanyard that always gets caught on the corner of the counter. It's one of those lanyards with the company logo printed on it, but the letters are all peeling off so it just looks like a bunch of random white flakes. It kind of reminds me of dandruff, which is so gross. I had this teacher in high school, Mr. Henderson, who wore these dark navy sweaters every single day, and his shoulders were always covered in it. We used to sit in the back row and count how many times he would scratch his head during a lecture. He taught geometry, which I was terrible at anyway. I could never remember all the different formulas for the triangles. I mean, when are you ever going to need to know the hypotenuse of a right triangle in real life? Unless you're building a roof or something, I guess. My uncle built his own shed in his backyard once, but he didn't measure anything right and the door wouldn't even close. He had to use a bungee cord to keep it shut.

But yeah, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, it was at work where I was doing that, just standing there with the key and saying the numbers instead of doing my job.

Um, well... wait, do I remember it? I mean, I think I remember parts of it, but mostly I just remember people looking at me weird. Like, I told you about what scared everyone at work, right? How I'd just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or doing things totally out of order. Like I'd be standing there by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. 

The checks at our restaurant are printed on this really flimsy paper that always jams the receipt printer. Dave refuses to call the repair guy because he says it costs too much just for them to come out and look at it. So we always have to open up the top of the machine and pull out these long, crinkled ribbons of paper. It reminds me of those streamer things we used to decorate the gym with for middle school dances. We had this one dance in eighth grade where the theme was "Under the Sea," and they hung all this blue and green crepe paper from the ceiling to look like seaweed. But the air conditioning was broken, so it was super humid in there, and the dye from the paper started dripping onto the floor. Everyone's white sneakers got totally ruined. I was wearing these brand new Keds that my mom had just bought me for back-to-school, and she was so mad. She tried bleaching them, but they just turned this weird yellowish-green color. I had to wear them for the rest of the semester anyway, and this kid named Tyler kept calling me "Swamp Foot." Kids can be so mean, you know? Tyler actually ended up moving away in high school because his dad got transferred for work. I think they moved to Ohio or something. 

But um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, so I'd just be standing there doing that instead of closing checks, and people would get really freaked out. I don't really know if there's a blank spot or not, I just know that's what I was doing.

It always pulls the little hairs on my wrist, so I refuse to wear it, I just hold it in my hand. My old manager, Dave, he used to make us wear them on our wrists no matter what, but he transferred to the Peoria location last year. I actually lived in Peoria for a bit when I was taking classes at the community college there. I was going to do early childhood education, but the drive was just too far in the winter. Do you get a lot of snow where you live? Because driving in it is just the worst, the salt ruins the undercarriage of your car. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah, the restaurant said I had to come see someone because of the... the freezing up thing, before they put me back on the schedule.\n\nDoctor: When you were freezing up and repeating those table numbers, were you aware of what was happening around you, or did it feel like you lost time or consciousness?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't know if I lost time or not. It's hard to tell because we aren't allowed to wear watches on the floor anyway. It's a health code thing, you know? They say bacteria gets trapped under the watch band. Which makes sense, I guess, especially if you get salad dressing on it. Ranch dressing is the absolute worst to wash out of things. We make ours from scratch in the back, in these giant plastic buckets, and the buttermilk smell just lingers on your apron forever. \n\nMy mom used to use buttermilk to make biscuits every Sunday, but she used the canned ones. You know, the kind that pop when you peel the paper back? I always hated opening those, the popping sound always made me jump so bad. I'd always make my brother do it. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, being aware. Yeah, I don't know, I just remember standing there with the key. I don't really know what was happening around me.\n\nDoctor: During these freezing episodes, did you feel disconnected from your body, like you were watching yourself from outside, or did things around you feel unreal or dreamlike?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I didn't feel like I was outside my body or anything. And it wasn't really like a dream, either. I actually don't dream very much anyway, or at least I never remember them when I wake up. \n\nMy roommate, Chloe, she remembers all of hers in like, crazy detail. She actually keeps this dream journal by her bed with a little tiny padlock on it, like we're still in middle school or something. It's one of those fuzzy notebooks, you know? I think she got it at Claire's at the mall. I haven't been to the mall in forever, honestly. The parking there is just such a total nightmare, especially on the weekends. People just leave their shopping carts right in the middle of the empty spots instead of pushing them to the little return corrals, which is so incredibly lazy. I always put my cart back because the cart guys work hard enough as it is. \n\nBut, um... wait, did you ask about my body? Yeah, no, I was just in it. I didn't feel disconnected or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze up and repeat those table numbers, does it happen at random times, or have you noticed it happens more often in certain situations or around certain things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if it's random or not. I haven't really noticed a pattern, I guess. I mean, it happened that one time by the soda station, but the soda station is always so chaotic anyway. People are always bumping into you trying to get ice. We have this one machine that dispenses crushed ice instead of cubed, and everyone fights over it because the customers always specifically ask for crushed ice in their iced teas. \n\nI actually prefer crushed ice myself, especially when I'm drinking lemonade. My grandma used to make the best homemade lemonade in the summer. She had this huge lemon tree in her backyard, and we'd spend hours picking them. She had this weird little metal contraption to squeeze them, and it always pinched my fingers if I wasn't careful. She kept it in this drawer with all her other random kitchen gadgets, like an apple corer and a melon baller. Do people even use melon ballers anymore? I feel like you only ever see those fancy fruit salads at weddings or maybe baby showers. \n\nMy cousin just had a baby shower last month, and they had this huge fruit carving that looked like a bassinet. It was actually kind of creepy looking, to be honest. The baby's head was made out of a cantaloupe. \n\nBut, um... yeah, I don't know about the freezing thing. I guess it just happens whenever. I haven't really paid attention to what's going on around me when it starts.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that manager Dave used to make you wear the coiled bracelet on your wrist, and that it pulls your hairs and bothers you. Have you noticed if the freezing episodes happen more when you see that bracelet, or when you're reminded of Dave, or does it seem completely unrelated to that?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think it has anything to do with Dave, or the bracelet. I mean, the bracelet is just really annoying. It's one of those plastic coil ones, kind of like an old telephone cord? We had this bright yellow phone on the wall in our kitchen growing up, with a cord that stretched all the way into the living room so my mom could watch TV while she talked on it. She loved watching those daytime soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having secret evil twins. I always thought it was so funny how the actors would just stare off into the distance dramatically before the commercial breaks. \n\nLike, nobody actually does that in real life. Unless they're trying to read a menu that's really far away, I guess. We have this big chalkboard menu above the register at work, and the chalk markers they use are so faded you can barely read the daily soup specials. I keep telling Dave we need to buy new ones, but he's super cheap about office supplies. He won't even buy the good pens, just those cheap clear plastic ones where the ink gets all clumpy at the tip and smears all over your hands. If you get that black ink on your uniform shirt, it is never coming out, no matter how much hairspray you use on it. People always say hairspray gets out ink, but I think that's just an old wives' tale. My aunt used to swear by club soda for stains, but club soda just tastes like angry water to me. \n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing. Yeah, I haven't noticed it happening more around Dave or when I look at the bracelet or anything. It doesn't really seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze up and repeat the table numbers, does it always happen at work, or have you had these episodes happen in other places like at home or when you're out doing other things?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's mostly at work, I think. That's actually what scared everyone there, honestly. I would just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or I'd do things totally out of order. Like, I'd be standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nThe cash drawer key is on this really long, annoying lanyard that always gets caught on the corner of the counter. It's one of those lanyards with the company logo printed on it, but the letters are all peeling off so it just looks like a bunch of random white flakes. It kind of reminds me of dandruff, which is so gross. I had this teacher in high school, Mr. Henderson, who wore these dark navy sweaters every single day, and his shoulders were always covered in it. We used to sit in the back row and count how many times he would scratch his head during a lecture. He taught geometry, which I was terrible at anyway. I could never remember all the different formulas for the triangles. I mean, when are you ever going to need to know the hypotenuse of a right triangle in real life? Unless you're building a roof or something, I guess. My uncle built his own shed in his backyard once, but he didn't measure anything right and the door wouldn't even close. He had to use a bungee cord to keep it shut.\n\nBut yeah, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, it was at work where I was doing that, just standing there with the key and saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: After one of these freezing episodes ends, can you remember what you were doing and saying during it, or is there a blank spot where you just don't know what happened?\n\nPatient: Um, well... wait, do I remember it? I mean, I think I remember parts of it, but mostly I just remember people looking at me weird. Like, I told you about what scared everyone at work, right? How I'd just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or doing things totally out of order. Like I'd be standing there by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nThe checks at our restaurant are printed on this really flimsy paper that always jams the receipt printer. Dave refuses to call the repair guy because he says it costs too much just for them to come out and look at it. So we always have to open up the top of the machine and pull out these long, crinkled ribbons of paper. It reminds me of those streamer things we used to decorate the gym with for middle school dances. We had this one dance in eighth grade where the theme was \"Under the Sea,\" and they hung all this blue and green crepe paper from the ceiling to look like seaweed. But the air conditioning was broken, so it was super humid in there, and the dye from the paper started dripping onto the floor. Everyone's white sneakers got totally ruined. I was wearing these brand new Keds that my mom had just bought me for back-to-school, and she was so mad. She tried bleaching them, but they just turned this weird yellowish-green color. I had to wear them for the rest of the semester anyway, and this kid named Tyler kept calling me \"Swamp Foot.\" Kids can be so mean, you know? Tyler actually ended up moving away in high school because his dad got transferred for work. I think they moved to Ohio or something. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, so I'd just be standing there doing that instead of closing checks, and people would get really freaked out. I don't really know if there's a blank spot or not, I just know that's what I was doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I was wearing these brand new Keds that my mom had just bought me for back-to-school, and she was so mad. She tried bleaching them, but they just turned this weird yellowish-green color. I had to wear them for the rest of the semester anyway, and this kid named Tyler kept calling me \"Swamp Foot.\" Kids can be so mean, you know? Tyler actually ended up moving away in high school because his dad got transferred for work. I think they moved to Ohio or something. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, so I'd just be standing there doing that instead of closing checks, and people would get really freaked out. I don't really know if there's a blank spot or not, I just know that's what I was doing.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you were standing by the soda station repeating those table numbers, can you actually remember saying those numbers, or do people just tell you that you were saying them but you have no memory of it yourself?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, like I was just saying, that's what really scared everyone at work so much. I don't think I actually remember hearing my own voice say them. They had to tell me afterward that I was doing things completely out of order, just standing there frozen by the soda station holding the cash key instead of closing out the checks. 

The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. Especially the root beer nozzle. Nobody ever takes the little plastic caps off to soak them in soda water at the end of the shift like you're supposed to. Dave put up this huge laminated sign in the breakroom about it, but people just ignore it. The laminating machine he used for it smells exactly like burning hair when it gets hot. I tried to use it once to laminate a recipe card my grandma gave me for her famous meatloaf so it wouldn't get grease stains on it. She used to put brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top, which sounds kind of gross, but it gets all caramelized and crispy on the edges in the oven. We used to eat it every Sunday while watching football, even though I don't really understand football at all. There are just too many rules, and the referees are always throwing those little yellow flags everywhere. It reminds me of when you drop a yellow napkin on the floor at the restaurant and someone just steps on it instead of picking it up. People are so lazy nowadays. Like, how hard is it to just bend down and pick up a piece of paper? 

But, um... wait, what were you asking about the numbers? Oh, right, if I remember saying them. No, I think the other servers just told me I was repeating them while I was stuck there. I didn't realize I was doing it.

People just leave their shopping carts right in the middle of the empty spots instead of pushing them to the little return corrals, which is so incredibly lazy. I always put my cart back because the cart guys work hard enough as it is. \n\nBut, um... wait, did you ask about my body? Yeah, no, I was just in it. I didn't feel disconnected or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze up and repeat those table numbers, does it happen at random times, or have you noticed it happens more often in certain situations or around certain things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if it's random or not. I haven't really noticed a pattern, I guess. I mean, it happened that one time by the soda station, but the soda station is always so chaotic anyway. People are always bumping into you trying to get ice. We have this one machine that dispenses crushed ice instead of cubed, and everyone fights over it because the customers always specifically ask for crushed ice in their iced teas. \n\nI actually prefer crushed ice myself, especially when I'm drinking lemonade. My grandma used to make the best homemade lemonade in the summer. She had this huge lemon tree in her backyard, and we'd spend hours picking them. She had this weird little metal contraption to squeeze them, and it always pinched my fingers if I wasn't careful. She kept it in this drawer with all her other random kitchen gadgets, like an apple corer and a melon baller. Do people even use melon ballers anymore? I feel like you only ever see those fancy fruit salads at weddings or maybe baby showers. \n\nMy cousin just had a baby shower last month, and they had this huge fruit carving that looked like a bassinet. It was actually kind of creepy looking, to be honest. The baby's head was made out of a cantaloupe. \n\nBut, um... yeah, I don't know about the freezing thing. I guess it just happens whenever. I haven't really paid attention to what's going on around me when it starts.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that manager Dave used to make you wear the coiled bracelet on your wrist, and that it pulls your hairs and bothers you. Have you noticed if the freezing episodes happen more when you see that bracelet, or when you're reminded of Dave, or does it seem completely unrelated to that?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think it has anything to do with Dave, or the bracelet. I mean, the bracelet is just really annoying. It's one of those plastic coil ones, kind of like an old telephone cord? We had this bright yellow phone on the wall in our kitchen growing up, with a cord that stretched all the way into the living room so my mom could watch TV while she talked on it. She loved watching those daytime soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having secret evil twins. I always thought it was so funny how the actors would just stare off into the distance dramatically before the commercial breaks. \n\nLike, nobody actually does that in real life. Unless they're trying to read a menu that's really far away, I guess. We have this big chalkboard menu above the register at work, and the chalk markers they use are so faded you can barely read the daily soup specials. I keep telling Dave we need to buy new ones, but he's super cheap about office supplies. He won't even buy the good pens, just those cheap clear plastic ones where the ink gets all clumpy at the tip and smears all over your hands. If you get that black ink on your uniform shirt, it is never coming out, no matter how much hairspray you use on it. People always say hairspray gets out ink, but I think that's just an old wives' tale. My aunt used to swear by club soda for stains, but club soda just tastes like angry water to me. \n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing. Yeah, I haven't noticed it happening more around Dave or when I look at the bracelet or anything. It doesn't really seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze up and repeat the table numbers, does it always happen at work, or have you had these episodes happen in other places like at home or when you're out doing other things?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's mostly at work, I think. That's actually what scared everyone there, honestly. I would just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or I'd do things totally out of order. Like, I'd be standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nThe cash drawer key is on this really long, annoying lanyard that always gets caught on the corner of the counter. It's one of those lanyards with the company logo printed on it, but the letters are all peeling off so it just looks like a bunch of random white flakes. It kind of reminds me of dandruff, which is so gross. I had this teacher in high school, Mr. Henderson, who wore these dark navy sweaters every single day, and his shoulders were always covered in it. We used to sit in the back row and count how many times he would scratch his head during a lecture. He taught geometry, which I was terrible at anyway. I could never remember all the different formulas for the triangles. I mean, when are you ever going to need to know the hypotenuse of a right triangle in real life? Unless you're building a roof or something, I guess. My uncle built his own shed in his backyard once, but he didn't measure anything right and the door wouldn't even close. He had to use a bungee cord to keep it shut.\n\nBut yeah, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, it was at work where I was doing that, just standing there with the key and saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: After one of these freezing episodes ends, can you remember what you were doing and saying during it, or is there a blank spot where you just don't know what happened?\n\nPatient: Um, well... wait, do I remember it? I mean, I think I remember parts of it, but mostly I just remember people looking at me weird. Like, I told you about what scared everyone at work, right? How I'd just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or doing things totally out of order. Like I'd be standing there by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nThe checks at our restaurant are printed on this really flimsy paper that always jams the receipt printer. Dave refuses to call the repair guy because he says it costs too much just for them to come out and look at it. So we always have to open up the top of the machine and pull out these long, crinkled ribbons of paper. It reminds me of those streamer things we used to decorate the gym with for middle school dances. We had this one dance in eighth grade where the theme was \"Under the Sea,\" and they hung all this blue and green crepe paper from the ceiling to look like seaweed. But the air conditioning was broken, so it was super humid in there, and the dye from the paper started dripping onto the floor. Everyone's white sneakers got totally ruined. I was wearing these brand new Keds that my mom had just bought me for back-to-school, and she was so mad. She tried bleaching them, but they just turned this weird yellowish-green color. I had to wear them for the rest of the semester anyway, and this kid named Tyler kept calling me \"Swamp Foot.\" Kids can be so mean, you know? Tyler actually ended up moving away in high school because his dad got transferred for work. I think they moved to Ohio or something. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, so I'd just be standing there doing that instead of closing checks, and people would get really freaked out. I don't really know if there's a blank spot or not, I just know that's what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: When you were standing by the soda station repeating those table numbers, can you actually remember saying those numbers, or do people just tell you that you were saying them but you have no memory of it yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was just saying, that's what really scared everyone at work so much. I don't think I actually remember hearing my own voice say them. They had to tell me afterward that I was doing things completely out of order, just standing there frozen by the soda station holding the cash key instead of closing out the checks. \n\nThe soda station is always so sticky, by the way. Especially the root beer nozzle. Nobody ever takes the little plastic caps off to soak them in soda water at the end of the shift like you're supposed to. Dave put up this huge laminated sign in the breakroom about it, but people just ignore it. The laminating machine he used for it smells exactly like burning hair when it gets hot. I tried to use it once to laminate a recipe card my grandma gave me for her famous meatloaf so it wouldn't get grease stains on it. She used to put brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top, which sounds kind of gross, but it gets all caramelized and crispy on the edges in the oven. We used to eat it every Sunday while watching football, even though I don't really understand football at all. There are just too many rules, and the referees are always throwing those little yellow flags everywhere. It reminds me of when you drop a yellow napkin on the floor at the restaurant and someone just steps on it instead of picking it up. People are so lazy nowadays. Like, how hard is it to just bend down and pick up a piece of paper? \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking about the numbers? Oh, right, if I remember saying them. No, I think the other servers just told me I was repeating them while I was stuck there. I didn't realize I was doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, well, I guess it felt completely new? Or maybe I just wasn't paying attention, I don't really know. Like I keep saying, the whole thing just really scared everyone at work because I'd be right in the middle of service and just... freeze up, doing things totally out of order.

My cat, Barnaby, caught a mouse once and brought it right into my bedroom. I was so freaked out. Barnaby is an orange tabby, and they say on the internet that orange cats all share one brain cell, which is honestly so true. He literally stares at the blank wall for hours. Sometimes he tries to eat plastic grocery bags. The vet said he probably just likes the crinkly sound, but it costs like fifty dollars just to take him in for a basic checkup to hear that, which is ridiculous. Vets are such a rip-off. My friend Sarah took her dog to get his teeth cleaned and it was like four hundred dollars. Can you believe that? 

But, um... wait, what was your question? Oh, if I knew I was doing it. Yeah, no, I had no idea. I was just standing there by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, repeating those table numbers instead of actually closing out my checks. I guess that's why everyone got so freaked out about it.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF44.5, F48.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, and welcome. I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. Thank you for coming in today. What brings you to the clinic?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Um, well, my boss kind of made me come. It's just... what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service. Or, like, doing things totally out of order? The other day I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nWhich is annoying because I usually always close my checks right away. But that cash drawer key, it has this really tight coiled plastic bracelet on it, you know the kind? Like a telephone cord. It always pulls the little hairs on my wrist, so I refuse to wear it, I just hold it in my hand. My old manager, Dave, he used to make us wear them on our wrists no matter what, but he transferred to the Peoria location last year. I actually lived in Peoria for a bit when I was taking classes at the community college there. I was going to do early childhood education, but the drive was just too far in the winter. Do you get a lot of snow where you live? Because driving in it is just the worst, the salt ruins the undercarriage of your car. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah, the restaurant said I had to come see someone because of the... the freezing up thing, before they put me back on the schedule.\n\nDoctor: When you were freezing up and repeating those table numbers, were you aware of what was happening around you, or did it feel like you lost time or consciousness?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I don't know if I lost time or not. It's hard to tell because we aren't allowed to wear watches on the floor anyway. It's a health code thing, you know? They say bacteria gets trapped under the watch band. Which makes sense, I guess, especially if you get salad dressing on it. Ranch dressing is the absolute worst to wash out of things. We make ours from scratch in the back, in these giant plastic buckets, and the buttermilk smell just lingers on your apron forever. \n\nMy mom used to use buttermilk to make biscuits every Sunday, but she used the canned ones. You know, the kind that pop when you peel the paper back? I always hated opening those, the popping sound always made me jump so bad. I'd always make my brother do it. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, being aware. Yeah, I don't know, I just remember standing there with the key. I don't really know what was happening around me.\n\nDoctor: During these freezing episodes, did you feel disconnected from your body, like you were watching yourself from outside, or did things around you feel unreal or dreamlike?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I didn't feel like I was outside my body or anything. And it wasn't really like a dream, either. I actually don't dream very much anyway, or at least I never remember them when I wake up. \n\nMy roommate, Chloe, she remembers all of hers in like, crazy detail. She actually keeps this dream journal by her bed with a little tiny padlock on it, like we're still in middle school or something. It's one of those fuzzy notebooks, you know? I think she got it at Claire's at the mall. I haven't been to the mall in forever, honestly. The parking there is just such a total nightmare, especially on the weekends. People just leave their shopping carts right in the middle of the empty spots instead of pushing them to the little return corrals, which is so incredibly lazy. I always put my cart back because the cart guys work hard enough as it is. \n\nBut, um... wait, did you ask about my body? Yeah, no, I was just in it. I didn't feel disconnected or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze up and repeat those table numbers, does it happen at random times, or have you noticed it happens more often in certain situations or around certain things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if it's random or not. I haven't really noticed a pattern, I guess. I mean, it happened that one time by the soda station, but the soda station is always so chaotic anyway. People are always bumping into you trying to get ice. We have this one machine that dispenses crushed ice instead of cubed, and everyone fights over it because the customers always specifically ask for crushed ice in their iced teas. \n\nI actually prefer crushed ice myself, especially when I'm drinking lemonade. My grandma used to make the best homemade lemonade in the summer. She had this huge lemon tree in her backyard, and we'd spend hours picking them. She had this weird little metal contraption to squeeze them, and it always pinched my fingers if I wasn't careful. She kept it in this drawer with all her other random kitchen gadgets, like an apple corer and a melon baller. Do people even use melon ballers anymore? I feel like you only ever see those fancy fruit salads at weddings or maybe baby showers. \n\nMy cousin just had a baby shower last month, and they had this huge fruit carving that looked like a bassinet. It was actually kind of creepy looking, to be honest. The baby's head was made out of a cantaloupe. \n\nBut, um... yeah, I don't know about the freezing thing. I guess it just happens whenever. I haven't really paid attention to what's going on around me when it starts.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that manager Dave used to make you wear the coiled bracelet on your wrist, and that it pulls your hairs and bothers you. Have you noticed if the freezing episodes happen more when you see that bracelet, or when you're reminded of Dave, or does it seem completely unrelated to that?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think it has anything to do with Dave, or the bracelet. I mean, the bracelet is just really annoying. It's one of those plastic coil ones, kind of like an old telephone cord? We had this bright yellow phone on the wall in our kitchen growing up, with a cord that stretched all the way into the living room so my mom could watch TV while she talked on it. She loved watching those daytime soap operas, the ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having secret evil twins. I always thought it was so funny how the actors would just stare off into the distance dramatically before the commercial breaks. \n\nLike, nobody actually does that in real life. Unless they're trying to read a menu that's really far away, I guess. We have this big chalkboard menu above the register at work, and the chalk markers they use are so faded you can barely read the daily soup specials. I keep telling Dave we need to buy new ones, but he's super cheap about office supplies. He won't even buy the good pens, just those cheap clear plastic ones where the ink gets all clumpy at the tip and smears all over your hands. If you get that black ink on your uniform shirt, it is never coming out, no matter how much hairspray you use on it. People always say hairspray gets out ink, but I think that's just an old wives' tale. My aunt used to swear by club soda for stains, but club soda just tastes like angry water to me. \n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing. Yeah, I haven't noticed it happening more around Dave or when I look at the bracelet or anything. It doesn't really seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze up and repeat the table numbers, does it always happen at work, or have you had these episodes happen in other places like at home or when you're out doing other things?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's mostly at work, I think. That's actually what scared everyone there, honestly. I would just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or I'd do things totally out of order. Like, I'd be standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nThe cash drawer key is on this really long, annoying lanyard that always gets caught on the corner of the counter. It's one of those lanyards with the company logo printed on it, but the letters are all peeling off so it just looks like a bunch of random white flakes. It kind of reminds me of dandruff, which is so gross. I had this teacher in high school, Mr. Henderson, who wore these dark navy sweaters every single day, and his shoulders were always covered in it. We used to sit in the back row and count how many times he would scratch his head during a lecture. He taught geometry, which I was terrible at anyway. I could never remember all the different formulas for the triangles. I mean, when are you ever going to need to know the hypotenuse of a right triangle in real life? Unless you're building a roof or something, I guess. My uncle built his own shed in his backyard once, but he didn't measure anything right and the door wouldn't even close. He had to use a bungee cord to keep it shut.\n\nBut yeah, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, it was at work where I was doing that, just standing there with the key and saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: After one of these freezing episodes ends, can you remember what you were doing and saying during it, or is there a blank spot where you just don't know what happened?\n\nPatient: Um, well... wait, do I remember it? I mean, I think I remember parts of it, but mostly I just remember people looking at me weird. Like, I told you about what scared everyone at work, right? How I'd just start freezing up right in the middle of service, or doing things totally out of order. Like I'd be standing there by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. \n\nThe checks at our restaurant are printed on this really flimsy paper that always jams the receipt printer. Dave refuses to call the repair guy because he says it costs too much just for them to come out and look at it. So we always have to open up the top of the machine and pull out these long, crinkled ribbons of paper. It reminds me of those streamer things we used to decorate the gym with for middle school dances. We had this one dance in eighth grade where the theme was \"Under the Sea,\" and they hung all this blue and green crepe paper from the ceiling to look like seaweed. But the air conditioning was broken, so it was super humid in there, and the dye from the paper started dripping onto the floor. Everyone's white sneakers got totally ruined. I was wearing these brand new Keds that my mom had just bought me for back-to-school, and she was so mad. She tried bleaching them, but they just turned this weird yellowish-green color. I had to wear them for the rest of the semester anyway, and this kid named Tyler kept calling me \"Swamp Foot.\" Kids can be so mean, you know? Tyler actually ended up moving away in high school because his dad got transferred for work. I think they moved to Ohio or something. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, so I'd just be standing there doing that instead of closing checks, and people would get really freaked out. I don't really know if there's a blank spot or not, I just know that's what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: When you were standing by the soda station repeating those table numbers, can you actually remember saying those numbers, or do people just tell you that you were saying them but you have no memory of it yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was just saying, that's what really scared everyone at work so much. I don't think I actually remember hearing my own voice say them. They had to tell me afterward that I was doing things completely out of order, just standing there frozen by the soda station holding the cash key instead of closing out the checks. \n\nThe soda station is always so sticky, by the way. Especially the root beer nozzle. Nobody ever takes the little plastic caps off to soak them in soda water at the end of the shift like you're supposed to. Dave put up this huge laminated sign in the breakroom about it, but people just ignore it. The laminating machine he used for it smells exactly like burning hair when it gets hot. I tried to use it once to laminate a recipe card my grandma gave me for her famous meatloaf so it wouldn't get grease stains on it. She used to put brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top, which sounds kind of gross, but it gets all caramelized and crispy on the edges in the oven. We used to eat it every Sunday while watching football, even though I don't really understand football at all. There are just too many rules, and the referees are always throwing those little yellow flags everywhere. It reminds me of when you drop a yellow napkin on the floor at the restaurant and someone just steps on it instead of picking it up. People are so lazy nowadays. Like, how hard is it to just bend down and pick up a piece of paper? \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking about the numbers? Oh, right, if I remember saying them. No, I think the other servers just told me I was repeating them while I was stuck there. I didn't realize I was doing it.\n\nDoctor: When your coworkers told you that you were repeating those table numbers by the soda station, did that feel completely new to you—like you had no idea you had done that at all—or did it feel more like you just hadn't been paying close attention to what you were doing?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess it felt completely new? Or maybe I just wasn't paying attention, I don't really know. Like I keep saying, the whole thing just really scared everyone at work because I'd be right in the middle of service and just... freeze up, doing things totally out of order. \n\nSpeaking of paying attention, Dave is always complaining that nobody pays attention to the side-work chart. He bought this big whiteboard for the kitchen, but the dry erase markers he gets are so cheap and they squeak really loud when you write with them. It sounds exactly like a mouse. My cat, Barnaby, caught a mouse once and brought it right into my bedroom. I was so freaked out. Barnaby is an orange tabby, and they say on the internet that orange cats all share one brain cell, which is honestly so true. He literally stares at the blank wall for hours. Sometimes he tries to eat plastic grocery bags. The vet said he probably just likes the crinkly sound, but it costs like fifty dollars just to take him in for a basic checkup to hear that, which is ridiculous. Vets are such a rip-off. My friend Sarah took her dog to get his teeth cleaned and it was like four hundred dollars. Can you believe that? \n\nBut, um... wait, what was your question? Oh, if I knew I was doing it. Yeah, no, I had no idea. I was just standing there by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, repeating those table numbers instead of actually closing out my checks. I guess that's why everyone got so freaked out about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   "intent": "Determine whether the patient has true dissociative amnesia (complete memory loss of the episode) versus pseudo-seizure/conversion disorder (awareness but loss of voluntary control). This is the key differentiator between F44.5 (Dissociative Amnesia) and F48.1 (Depersonalization/Derealization Disorder or other specified somatic symptom disorder). I was wearing these brand new Keds that my mom had just bought me for back-to-school, and she was so mad. She tried bleaching them, but they just turned this weird yellowish-green color. I had to wear them for the rest of the semester anyway, and this kid named Tyler kept calling me \"Swamp Foot.\" Kids can be so mean, you know? Tyler actually ended up moving away in high school because his dad got transferred for work. I think they moved to Ohio or something. \n\nBut um, what was I saying? Oh, right, the freezing thing. Yeah, so I'd just be standing there doing that instead of closing checks, and people would get really freaked out. I don't really know if there's a blank spot or not, I just know that's what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: When you were standing by the soda station repeating those table numbers, can you actually remember saying those numbers, or do people just tell you that you were saying them but you have no memory of it yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was just saying, that's what really scared everyone at work so much. I don't think I actually remember hearing my own voice say them. They had to tell me afterward that I was doing things completely out of order, just standing there frozen by the soda station holding the cash key instead of closing out the checks. \n\nThe soda station is always so sticky, by the way. Especially the root beer nozzle. Nobody ever takes the little plastic caps off to soak them in soda water at the end of the shift like you're supposed to. Dave put up this huge laminated sign in the breakroom about it, but people just ignore it. The laminating machine he used for it smells exactly like burning hair when it gets hot. I tried to use it once to laminate a recipe card my grandma gave me for her famous meatloaf so it wouldn't get grease stains on it. She used to put brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top, which sounds kind of gross, but it gets all caramelized and crispy on the edges in the oven. We used to eat it every Sunday while watching football, even though I don't really understand football at all. There are just too many rules, and the referees are always throwing those little yellow flags everywhere. It reminds me of when you drop a yellow napkin on the floor at the restaurant and someone just steps on it instead of picking it up. People are so lazy nowadays. Like, how hard is it to just bend down and pick up a piece of paper? \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking about the numbers? Oh, right, if I remember saying them. No, I think the other servers just told me I was repeating them while I was stuck there. I didn't realize I was doing it.\n\nDoctor: When your coworkers told you that you were repeating those table numbers by the soda station, did that feel completely new to you—like you had no idea you had done that at all—or did it feel more like you just hadn't been paying close attention to what you were doing?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess it felt completely new? Or maybe I just wasn't paying attention, I don't really know. Like I keep saying, the whole thing just really scared everyone at work because I'd be right in the middle of service and just... freeze up, doing things totally out of order. \n\nSpeaking of paying attention, Dave is always complaining that nobody pays attention to the side-work chart. He bought this big whiteboard for the kitchen, but the dry erase markers he gets are so cheap and they squeak really loud when you write with them. It sounds exactly like a mouse. My cat, Barnaby, caught a mouse once and brought it right into my bedroom. I was so freaked out. Barnaby is an orange tabby, and they say on the internet that orange cats all share one brain cell, which is honestly so true. He literally stares at the blank wall for hours. Sometimes he tries to eat plastic grocery bags. The vet said he probably just likes the crinkly sound, but it costs like fifty dollars just to take him in for a basic checkup to hear that, which is ridiculous. Vets are such a rip-off. Like that time period just wasn't there. And I know I've told you this already, but that's exactly what scared everyone at work so much. I'd just freeze up right in the middle of service, completely doing things out of order. It constantly pinches your arm hair if you wear it on your wrist. I actually tried waxing my arms once because my sister told me it was a trend. She watches all these beauty tutorials on YouTube. There's this one girl she watches who does her makeup while talking about true crime. It's so weird, right? Like, \"Here's how to blend your blush, and also this guy robbed a bank and vanished.\" I don't get how people can sleep after watching that stuff. I watched one video about a creepy hotel in Los Angeles, and I had nightmares for a week about getting stuck in an elevator. Elevators make me so claustrophobic anyway, especially the one at my dentist's office. It shakes right before the doors open, and they always play that awful elevator music that sounds like a keyboard demo. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the memory part. It was just a blank spot.
